% Source CSV: tables_csv_original/E1_E6/table_E1_leakage_audit.csv
\begin{table*}[t]
\centering
\scriptsize
\begin{tabular}{llllllllll}
\toprule
Protocol & Window rule & Train windows & Val windows & Test windows & VCEA Skill$_{\mathrm{MSE}}$ & M1 Skill$_{\mathrm{MSE}}$ & VCEA gain vs M1 & SS-VCEA stress ratio & Conclusion \\
\midrule
Target-time assignment & 原始 target-time protocol：按目标时刻归属 split，history 允许跨 boundary，但不访问目标之后的时间帧。 & 1117.000 & 120.000 & 306.000 & 0.178 & 0.078 & 0.099 & 0.992 & positive gain retained \\
Full-window containment & history window 与所有 target horizon 均需完整落在同一 split。 & 1117.000 & 30.000 & 216.000 & 0.159 & 0.080 & 0.079 & 1.152 & positive gain retained \\
Gap-buffer (10 frames) & 在 split boundary 后增加 10 帧缓冲，窗口仍按 target-time 归属；这是对 full retrain 之外的更严格审计。 & 1057.000 & 60.000 & 246.000 & 0.170 & 0.085 & 0.085 & 1.133 & positive gain retained \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Leakage-audit comparison across the target-time assignment, full-window containment, and gap-buffer protocols. VCEA retains a positive gain over M1 under stricter split rules, although the number of admissible windows decreases and the margin is reduced. This supports the interpretation that the aggregation advantage is not an artifact of target-time assignment.}
\label{tab:e1_leakage_audit}
\end{table*}

without\_persistence & Linear-Seasonal, M1, C3\_opt & 0.176 & 0.334 & 1.088 & 5.000 & ablation / diagnostic \\

\caption{Expert-pool ablation on the main-six setting. The full VCEA pool outperforms removal variants, while uniform averaging is not a reliable substitute. \label{tab:e2_expert_ablation_main6}
\end{table*}

Uniform average & uniform & 0.000 & 0.000 & -1.805 & 0.267 & 4.112 & 2.002 \\

\bottomrule

\caption{Calibration ablation for validation-based expert aggregation. Full tile+horizon validation calibration yields the strongest main-six result and the smallest oracle gap, whereas uniform averaging is insufficient under the strong persistence baseline.}
\label{tab:e3_calibration_ablation}
\end{table*}

The table aggregates performance over six broad European regions, four audit-regime groups, and three coverage groups. \label{tab:e7_strong_failure_cases}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E1/fig_E1_skill_delta_by_tile.png}
\caption{Tile-wise VCEA gain over M1 under the E1 leakage-audit protocols. The gains remain positive under stricter protocols, supporting the interpretation that VCEA does not rely on target-time assignment artifacts.}
\label{fig:e1_skill_delta_by_tile}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E2/fig_E2_expert_weights_heatmap.png}
\caption{Validation-calibrated expert weights by tile and horizon. The heatmap shows that VCEA does not reduce to a uniform average and instead shifts trust across persistence, linear-seasonal, and neural experts according to tile-horizon conditions.}
\label{fig:e2_expert_weights}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E3/fig_E3_calibration_barplot.png}
\caption{Calibration-ablation summary across aggregation strategies. Full tile+horizon validation calibration achieves the strongest main-six skill and the smallest oracle gap, whereas uniform averaging is insufficient under the strong persistence baseline.}
\label{fig:e3_calibration_barplot}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E6/fig_E6_skill_stress_frontier.png}
\caption{Skill-stress frontier for the SS-VCEA threshold sweep. The selected $q97.5$ operating point keeps skill close to VCEA while reducing stress below one, supporting its use as the main conservative deployment setting.}
\label{fig:e6_skill_stress_frontier}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E7_strong/fig_E7_pan_europe_tile_map.png}
\caption{Geographically stratified pan-European frozen audit design. The map shows the development tiles, the original external tiles, and the 14 held-out EGMS 2019--2023 tiles used in the strong audit. This is a frozen audit rather than an exhaustive population estimate over all European deformation conditions.}
\label{fig:e7_pan_europe_tile_map}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E7_strong/fig_E7_skill_by_region.png}
\caption{Strong E7 skill by broad European region. Over the 14 held-out tiles and 56 tile-horizon evaluations, VCEA supports positive average transfer beyond the development and original external sets, while SS-VCEA preserves most of the gain with more conservative behavior.}
\label{fig:e7_skill_by_region}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E7_strong/fig_E7_skill_vs_coverage.png}
\caption{Strong E7 VCEA gain versus valid coverage across the 14 held-out tiles. The trend suggests that the transfer gain is not reducible to a simple valid-coverage effect, although lower-support cases still define important limitations.}
\label{fig:e7_skill_vs_coverage}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E1/fig_E1_protocol_timeline.png}
\caption{Timeline illustration of the three E1 split protocols. The figure contrasts the original target-time assignment, the stricter full-window containment rule, and the gap-buffer protocol used to reduce near-boundary leakage risk.}
\label{fig:e1_protocol_timeline}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E2/fig_E2_remove_expert_delta.png}
\caption{Performance change after removing individual experts from the VCEA pool. The figure indicates that the linear-seasonal and neural experts contribute complementary gains, while removing persistence can sharply worsen stress.}
\label{fig:e2_remove_expert_delta}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E3/fig_E3_val_vs_test_weights.png}
\caption{Comparison between validation-derived weights and oracle test weights. The agreement suggests that validation ranking stability is strong enough to support the static validation-calibrated aggregation scheme.}
\label{fig:e3_val_vs_test_weights}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E4/fig_E4_test_skill_by_block.png}
\caption{Blocked-validation test skill by calibration block. Positive transfer remains visible across blocks, but short blocks are less stable than the full validation period, which supports using the full validation span in the frozen protocol.}
\label{fig:e4_test_skill_by_block}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E4/fig_E4_weight_stability.png}
\caption{Weight stability under blocked validation. The figure shows how far blocked-validation weights drift from the full-validation weights used in the frozen protocol.}
\label{fig:e4_weight_stability}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E5/fig_E5_external_map_regime.png}
\caption{Map of the original external locked tiles with dominant-regime annotations. The finite external set is geographically useful but not sufficient for a Europe-wide population claim on its own.}
\label{fig:e5_external_map_regime}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E5/fig_E5_skill_vs_coverage.png}
\caption{External gain versus valid coverage in the original external audit. The positive transfer is not purely a valid-coverage artifact, because gains remain visible across different support levels.}
\label{fig:e5_skill_vs_coverage}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E6/fig_E6_fallback_rate_by_tile.png}
\caption{Fallback rate by tile under the SS-VCEA threshold sweep. The figure helps interpret where conservative fallback is triggered and how risk control varies across tiles.}
\label{fig:e6_fallback_rate_by_tile}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E7_strong/fig_E7_skill_by_regime.png}
\caption{Strong E7 performance grouped by the four audit-regime groups. The figure suggests that VCEA transfer extends beyond the original evaluation regions while still exhibiting regime-dependent limits that should be discussed as remaining failure modes.}
\label{fig:e7_skill_by_regime}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E7_strong/fig_E7_stress_by_region.png}
\caption{Strong E7 stress ratio and fallback rate by region for SS-VCEA. The figure supports the interpretation that the selected conservative wrapper reduces over-correction risk below one on average while retaining most of the VCEA gain.}
\label{fig:e7_stress_by_region}
\end{figure*}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/E7_strong/fig_E7_failure_case_overview.png}
\caption{Overview of strong E7 gains and stress across the 14 held-out EGMS 2019--2023 tiles and 56 tile-horizon evaluations. The remaining failures are localized rather than systematic, which supports a restrained claim of geographically stratified transfer.}
\label{fig:e7_failure_case_overview}
\end{figure*}
